Until one is chosen the row stays OPEN, and its severity is H rather than the C it inherits: the hole is real and reachable in the default configuration, and the mitigations — the derivation check, the loud log, the documented default — bound it without closing it **PARTLY 2026-09-23 — option (b) implemented, and the residue it does not reach is stated.** Decision taken with the operator: the configuration gate, not the per-block refusal. *The gate.* `bin/dwowd/src/main.rs` now refuses to start when an `mm_rpc` section is configured and no `monerod_url` is — the error naming the choice ("Set `monerod_url` (or pass `--monerod-rpc-url`), or remove the `mm_rpc` section to stop merging"). The combination "merge mining on, verifier off" is therefore unrepresentable rather than merely logged, and it fails at startup rather than as a later surprise: the check sits immediately after the finality config is final and before anything is opened. *Why the gate and not option (c), read rather than assumed.* `PowSource` is `Native | Monero(MoneroPowData)` (`src/linear/src/block.rs:36-41`), so merge-mined and natively-mined blocks are alternatives on one chain; refusing merge-mined blocks without a Monero node would make a default-configured node reject blocks the rest of the network accepts, which is a partition and not hardening. This cell's cost note for (b) is corrected in the same breath — (b) does **not** turn "a missing config into a node that cannot accept blocks", because a node without `mm_rpc` does not merge-mine and is untouched by the gate. *Measured, both ways.* Two runs, one config, `--network darkwow-devnet`, `[mm_rpc]` present: without the URL the gate's message is the last line and the process exits `ParseFailed("merge mining requires monerod_url")` **before** opening the database; with `--monerod-rpc-url` the gate is silent, `Monero RPC URL set via CLI` and `Starting DarkWow node...` are logged, and the run proceeds past it — into a database path this control made deliberately unopenable, so it touched no real database and bound no port. *Blast radius, checked rather than assumed.* The pipeline already kept this invariant and now has it enforced: `contrib/docker/darkwow-testnet/entrypoint.sh` writes the `mm_rpc` section only inside `if [ "$MERGE_MINING" = "true" ]` (`:245`, section at `:248`) and in that same branch defaults `MONEROD_RPC_URL` and writes `monerod_url` (`:258`, `:284`), so every pipeline node that merge-mines already sets it and no node that does not merge is affected. `contrib/docker/darkwow-testnet/reproduce_merge_mining.sh` did not — it injects `mm_rpc` and starts a monerod without pointing dwowd at it — so it now passes `--monerod-rpc-url` at its own monerod, which also means that script exercises the verified path instead of the unchecked one. A `darkwow-devnet`/`darkwow-testnet` node started from the shipped `dwowd_config.toml`, which enables `mm_rpc` for both, now needs the URL or must drop the section; that is the requirement the error states. *What remains, and why this is `PARTLY` and not `CLOSED`.* The gate bounds the **producer**, which is the configuration it can see. A node that does not merge-mine still receives merge-mined blocks from peers, and with `monerod_url` unset its only response remains the acceptor's warning at `block_acceptor.rs:232` naming what was not checked — so the proposition above holds for that node ("says plainly that it does not") while the hazard does not go away. The tail of this cell said *"Severity stays **H** for the reason it was H"*, which the raise at its head supersedes; those words are kept rather than rewritten, because the argument they make is the argument the raise rests on. The severity is **C**: the hole is still reachable in a configuration this change deliberately does not forbid, and it is not closable by configuration alone — that would mean making the Monero receipts verify without a Monero node, which is the derivation half `OBL-C67` has already closed, and separate work. **Two consequences of that gate, measured 2026-09-23, and neither was recorded when it landed.** The gate refuses `mm_rpc` without `monerod_url`; the shipped configuration enables `mm_rpc` for three networks and sets no `monerod_url`. So the *default* now refuses to start on exactly those three. `bin/dwowd/dwowd_config.toml` is not merely a sample — it is `const = include_str!("../dwowd_config.toml")` (`main.rs:46`), written out by the CLI on first run — so this is what every fresh node gets. The predicate reproduced over the file rather than recalled: `testnet`, `darkwow-devnet` and `darkwow-testnet` each have `mm_rpc` and no `monerod_url`, and `mainnet`/`localnet` have theirs commented out, so **the three networks that enable merge mining are the three that cannot start**. Dates settle the direction: those sections are from `2cd3b76f19` (2026-05-20), the gate from `3652157ca9` (2026-09-23) — a configuration that had started for four months is refused by the newer rule. It is not a *wrong* refusal (enabling merge mining without a verifier is what the gate exists to catch, and the error names both remedies), but it is a first-run experience no document mentions. **(ii) The documented bare-metal merge test can no longer pass.** `contrib/docker/darkwow-testnet/test_merge_mining_p2pool.sh` is cited as the E2E test in three documents and generates its config from that same template (`:406`), then starts the node with **no** `--monerod-rpc-url` (`:488`) — so the gate stops it before its RPC opens. Its sibling `reproduce_merge_mining.sh:85` was updated for this gate and passes the flag, with the reason in a comment; this one was missed. **What I did not do, and why**: I did not edit either the template or the script. The template's fix is a policy choice (should the shipped default enable merge mining at all?) that the gate's own message already makes actionable, and the script is a pipeline file whose repair I cannot test — `test_pipeline.sh` is not mine to run, and the memory rule is that pipeline files are edited for determinism, not to satisfy a new gate. So the repair is named rather than applied: **one flag, `--monerod-rpc-url`, mirroring `reproduce_merge_mining.sh:85`**, plus a decision on the template default **Both consequences repaired 2026-09-23, with the policy decided rather than deferred, plus a third consequence and a prior failure the first read missed.** *The template default — decided.* The shipped `bin/dwowd/dwowd_config.toml` no longer enables `mm_rpc` for any network: `testnet`, `darkwow-devnet` and `darkwow-testnet` are commented out in the same shape `mainnet` and `localnet` already used, each carrying a note that names `monerod_url` as the requirement and this row as the reason. Merge mining is opt-in — uncomment the section and supply the verifier, or pass `--monerod-rpc-url`. That is the answer to *"should the shipped default enable merge mining at all?"*: no, because the default is what a fresh node gets, and the gate's own message already treats the pairing as the invariant rather than a suggestion. *Control, run, and two-sided.* A release binary rebuilt from this tree, with `HOME` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` redirected into an empty directory: run 1 writes the template and exits with the documented *"Config file created … Please review it and try again."*, and the file it writes has **zero** uncommented `[network_config.*.mm_rpc]` sections; run 2 reaches `Initializing a DarkWow daemon...`; and the same written file with an `mm_rpc` section appended refuses with `merge mining requires monerod_url`. So removing the section is what stops the refusal, and the gate still bites when a section is present — the pair is what makes either single run evidence. *The script.* `contrib/docker/darkwow-testnet/test_merge_mining_p2pool.sh` no longer takes either half from the template: it appends its own `[network_config."darkwow-testnet".mm_rpc]` section, the shape `reproduce_merge_mining.sh:50-55` uses, and passes `--monerod-rpc-url` at the node start, so the E2E test three documents cite is back on the verified path rather than the unchecked one. Its sed range for the mm_rpc port went with it, because it edited a section that no longer exists. Verified by running that script's own config-generation commands against the new template and parsing the result — `mm_rpc`, the `database` override, `skip_sync`, `skip_fees` and the net overrides all land, and the file parses. *A third consequence, in a script this row did not name.* `contrib/docker/testnet-node/entrypoint.sh` has the same defect: in `MODE=merge` it writes an `mm_rpc` section and no `monerod_url`, then starts `/app/dwowd` with no flag. Filed as `OBL-C95`, not repaired here. *And the reason (i)'s conclusion was right while its cause was not.* The shipped default did not start for a reason that came **first**: `create_genesis` was missing from the template entirely, so the config never parsed and the gate above was never reached. Measured, fixed and controlled in the same pass as `OBL-C93`; the run described above is that control |

| OBL-C68 | **FIXED** | Anchor acquisition is off the consensus path. `stratum_submit` calls the blocking `anchor_block` directly inside the async handler **while `linear_submit_lock` is still held** (`stratum.rs:561`, guard taken at `:348`), so one slow ArDrive Turbo POST serialises every stratum submission — and the whole handler blocks the async executor thread while it waits. The built-in miner does this correctly via `smol::unblock` (`miner.rs:293-307`). `doc/src/arch/consensus/stratum.md:145` describes the anchor as "best-effort, non-blocking", which is false for this path. The delay is **bounded at 30 s by `ureq`'s `timeout_global`**, which is a wall-clock deadline and not the per-IO timeout an earlier version of this row called it: the crate documents it as "end-to-end, from DNS lookup to finishing reading the response body. Thus it covers all other timeouts" (`ureq-3.4.0/src/config.rs:663-669`), verified while fixing this row. So the hazard is 30 seconds of serialised submissions per stuck anchor, not an unbounded wait. **This is the one row in this residue with no characterization test**, deliberately: the lock scope is not observable without a stub Turbo server and a live handler, so the lock half is proven by the Stage 4 integration test — a second submission must complete while the first's anchor call is outstanding — and the deadline half needs no test, because it is the dependency's documented behaviour rather than ours | `stratum.rs:348`, `:561`; `caribina/anchor.rs:84-86` | H | **FIXED 2026-09-22; the behavioural half is still owed.** The lock half is verified by reading: `stratum.rs:631` `drop(submit_guard)` runs *before* the POST at `:633`, and the guard is re-taken at `:658` before `accept_block`; the comment there records both the 30-second bound and why the release is safe (an interleaved submission is rejected by `accept_block`'s own height check). The row's own test — a second submission completing while the first's anchor call is outstanding — remains the Stage 4 integration test, so this marker must not be read as behavioural verification |
| OBL-C69 | **FIXED** | A merge-mined block survives the **P2P broadcast** with the proof that makes it merge-mined. `BlockBroadcast`'s wire codec is `serde_json` in all three directions — `Encodable` (`linear_broadcast.rs:112`), `AsyncEncodable` (`:144`), `AsyncDecodable` (`:172`) — and `BlockHeader::pow_source` is declared `#[serde(default = "PowSource::native", skip)]` (`block.rs:131`), so `pow_source` is **never written and always decodes as `Native`**. A relayed merge-mined block therefore arrives reclassified as native, and `block_acceptor.rs:191` takes the *native* branch, verifying RandomX over a header xmrig never hashed — the exact check the Monero branch exists to replace, and one the block cannot satisfy. **Demonstrated** by `block_broadcast_wire_drops_pow_source` (`bin/dwowd/src/tests/wire_format.rs`): the encoded JSON carries no `pow_source` and the round-trip decodes to `Native`. The rejection itself is *inferred*, not demonstrated, and the register says which: `block_acceptor` is invoked with `BlockTarget::MAX` throughout the test harness, under which every hash passes, so the harness structurally cannot reproduce the failure it would cause on a chain with a real target. Following the mechanism through, a merge-mined block cannot propagate by broadcast at all; it reaches a peer only by the sync path, whose codec (`src/linear/src/serial_sync.rs:116-180`) encodes the discriminator correctly. **Three serializations of one consensus field now exist and only two preserve it**: the canonical one (correct), the storage path — `chain_state.rs:1135` uses `dwow_serialize`, and `chain_state.rs` contains **zero** occurrences of `serde_json` — and the wire, which does not. Note that the comment at `bin/dwowd/src/tests/merge_mining.rs:300-303` blames `serde_json` for storage and concludes the loss is a "pre-existing bug, tracked separately"; that comment is stale in its attribution (storage is fine) and was the only record of the real defect, which is on the wire | `linear_broadcast.rs:112`,`:144`,`:172`; `block.rs:131`; `block_acceptor.rs:191` | C | **FIXED 2026-09-22, and the characterization test is flipped.** `PowSource` gained a codec of its own (`serial_sync.rs:155`, `:168`) whose unknown-discriminator arm **errors** instead of returning `Native` (`:174-180`), plus `Serialize`/`Deserialize` impls that carry that codec's bytes (`:192`, `:199`); `block.rs`'s `#[serde(skip, default = "PowSource::native")]` is now `#[serde(default = "PowSource::native")]` (`block.rs:169-170`), so the wire and the canonical encoding cannot disagree. The demonstrating test was replaced by `block_broadcast_wire_carries_pow_source` (`wire_format.rs:123`), which round-trips a real `PowSource::Monero` through the actual JSON codec |
| OBL-C71 | **FIXED** | A `DataItem` decoded from untrusted bytes cannot panic its verifier. `DataItem::deserialize` (`caribina/data_item.rs:218`) accepts any buffer of at least `HEADER_LENGTH` (116) whose first two bytes are signature type 2, and validates nothing else. Two accessors then slice on `tag_bytes` — an unvalidated `u64` read straight from bytes 108..116 — and **which one panics depends on `tag_count`**: with `tag_count == 0`, `raw_tags` returns early without slicing and `raw_data` panics at `:149` on `bytes[116 + tag_bytes..]`; with `tag_count != 0`, `raw_tags` panics first at `:214` on `bytes[116..116 + tag_bytes]`. Both are "range end index out of range", both in release as well as debug, and both are reachable from `verify_signature` (`:193` builds its item list from `raw_tags` then `raw_data`). A `tag_bytes` near `u64::MAX` additionally overflows the `usize` addition in `data_start` (`:153`), panicking in debug and silently wrapping in release — in release `raw_data` then returns the wrong region, so the payload comparison reads attacker-chosen bytes. **Observed, not argued**: `test_data_item_hostile_tag_bytes_do_not_panic_the_verifier` exercises both branches and the panic is at `data_item.rs:149`; the test is written with `catch_unwind` so the effect is measured rather than described. **Currently reached only through the dead verifier, which is exactly why it must land before Stage 3 wires that verifier into consensus**: the plan's own design makes this path parse proof bytes carried in a block, at which point a single crafted block panics every node that accepts it. The six pre-existing tests in this file cannot see it: every one of them round-trips an item this code produced, so `tag_bytes` is always honest | `caribina/data_item.rs:149`, `:153`, `:193`, `:214`, `:218` | C | **FIXED 2026-09-22.** `DataItem::deserialize` now validates the declared tag area *before* constructing the item — `usize::try_from` on the `u64` (`:251`), then `checked_add` (`:254`) and a bound check against the buffer (`:257`) — so both previously-panicking accessor branches (`:149`, `:214`) and the near-`u64::MAX` `usize` overflow that silently wrapped in release are all unreachable. The test that asserted the panic is now the regression control that asserts rejection, `test_data_item_hostile_tag_bytes_are_rejected_not_panicked` (`:574`), and it covers all three hostile cases separately: `tag_count == 0`, `tag_count != 0`, and `tag_bytes == u64::MAX` |
| OBL-C70 | **FIXED** | `MAX_BLOCK_SIZE` bounds the size it claims to bound. `MAX_BLOCK_SIZE` is the block-wire cap ("single source of truth pinned across nodes, L1 barrier #7"), but both enforcement sites measure a `serde_json` encoding — `block_acceptor.rs:164` for acceptance and `linear_broadcast.rs:175` for the wire — and that encoding omits `pow_source` (`OBL-C69`). For a merge-mined block the measured length therefore excludes the entire `MoneroPowData`: the Monero block header, both merkle proofs, and a `Vec<u8>` `tx_extra`. This is **not** the concern the comment at `block_acceptor.rs:167-172` already addresses — that one is serde *version* drift, mitigated by a 1% margin; this is a systematic omission of a whole field, which a 1% margin does not cover. Severity is bounded by the same comment's own reasoning (`MAX_BLOCK_SIZE` is declared a DoS gate rather than a consensus rule), which is why this is M and not C | `block_acceptor.rs:164-181`; `linear_broadcast.rs:175`; `block.rs:131` | M | **FIXED 2026-09-22, as a consequence of `OBL-C69` rather than by its own change.** With `pow_source` no longer absent from serde, the acceptance measure — `serde_json::to_vec(block).len()` at `block_acceptor.rs:161-166` — now *includes* the whole `MoneroPowData`, which was the systematic omission this row objects to. The wire site measures the raw buffer (`linear_broadcast.rs:175`), which was always the true wire size. The serde-version margin the neighbouring comment describes is untouched and still the only remaining slack |

| OBL-C76 | **PARTLY** | A test that does not run in the configuration used to claim verification is not verification. Three mechanisms hide tests in this tree and they compound **(a fourth was found on 2026-09-23 — a module gated by its *parent* takes its `#[cfg(test)]` submodule with it, and the gate built for this row could not see it by walking files; see the evidence cell)**: **feature gates** — `src/linear/src/block.rs:703` declares `#[cfg(all(test, feature = "pow"))]`, so its entire test module is absent from a bare `cargo test -p dwow_chain --lib`, and `monero/mod.rs`'s `test_monero_powdata_serde` is `#[cfg(feature = "async")]`; **`#[ignore]`** — all eight Caribina integration tests; and the **name filters** in `heavyweight.sh`. Measured 2026-09-22: `cargo test -p dwow_chain --lib` lists **172** tests, `--all-features` lists **263**, and the 91 that differ are `validation` (26), `block` (23), `chain_state` (20), `consensus` (15), `sync_boundary` (4), `sync_connection` (1) and two Monero tests — precisely the consensus surface this campaign works on. **The gates are not affected**: `make test` runs `--release --all-features --workspace`, so `scripts/run-all-tests.sh` always exercised all 263. The defect is in the *ad-hoc verification command*, which is what one reaches for to check a single crate quickly — and this campaign's Stage 0 and Stage 1 commits both quoted `162 passed` from such a run, understating what had been verified and, for `chain_state.rs`, `block.rs` and the Monero receipts, claiming verification of tests that were never compiled. All pass under `--all-features` (re-measured the same day), so the findings stand and only the *claims* were under-run — which is the part worth pinning, because an unqualified pass count is indistinguishable from a run that skipped the test. A verification command must name its feature set | `block.rs:703`; `monero/mod.rs:484`; `caribina/integration_tests.rs` | M | **OPEN — it describes a verification *practice*, so it cannot be closed by a code change, only by a convention that a gate can enforce. Re-measured 2026-09-22, and it has not moved.** The three hiding mechanisms are all still present: `block.rs`'s test module is still `#[cfg(all(test, feature = "pow"))]` (`:703`), `monero/mod.rs`'s `test_monero_powdata_serde` is still `#[cfg(feature = "async")]`, and the Caribina integration tests are still `#[ignore]`d. The measurement is reproducible in one command — `cargo test -p dwow_chain --lib` lists **172** tests, `--all-features` lists **263** — and the 91 that differ are the consensus surface this campaign works on. What the row asks for is not a fix but a **convention**: every verification claim names its command and its feature set, which is a rule a reader follows and no test can enforce. This session is the first to apply it throughout — every command quoted in today's closures carries `--all-features` — and the practice still fails whenever someone writes "tests pass" without one, which is why the row stays open rather than being marked satisfied by improved behaviour. **Measured and mechanized 2026-09-23, and the measurement found a fourth mechanism plus nine sites this row did not name.** A gate now enumerates every test that does not run in its crate's *default* configuration and requires each to be declared in `script/hidden_test_exceptions.txt` with a reason and the command that runs it. **Twenty-nine sites, against the ten this row's prose named by file.** The mechanisms it named are all real and all present — the eight Caribina `#[ignore]`s, the two upstream-networking `#[ignore = "…"]`s (which the prose did not name), the four `pow`-gated test modules (`block`, `chain_state`, `consensus`, `validation`), and `monero/mod.rs`'s `async`-gated `test_monero_powdata_serde` — and **four sites were not in the row at all**: `monero/merkle_proof.rs`'s sibling serde test, and four `#[cfg(feature = "json")]` tests in `src/sdk/src/blockchain.rs:1221-1245` pinning serde transparency for the supply, fee, block-timestamp and Monero-height newtypes — the last of which is the field `OBL-C67` and `OBL-C80` are about. **The fourth mechanism was found by the gate's own first run, in its own coverage**: a module gated by its *parent* (`#[cfg(feature = "sync-p2p")] mod sync_boundary;` in `lib.rs`) takes its `#[cfg(test)]` submodule with it, and nothing inside the file can say so — walking files found 20 of the 29 and could not find the other 9, which include `sync_boundary` and `sync_connection` themselves. That is the five tests this row's own measurement attributed to `sync-p2p` without naming the gate, so the row had already seen the effect and not the cause. The remaining nine are `net/mod.rs`, `net/hosts.rs`, `net/known_hosts.rs`, `event_graph/mod.rs`, `util/pcg.rs`, `sdk/test_support.rs` (the module the heavyweight tests rely on is itself uncovered by a default sdk run) and `native_token`'s entrypoint module, whose gate is **negative** — `#[cfg(not(feature = "no-entrypoint"))]` — so it is present by default and absent under the `no-entrypoint` build the python SDK uses, which is a configuration someone can claim verification in. *The root cause, measured*: `src/linear/Cargo.toml` and `src/sdk/Cargo.toml` both declare `default = []`, and the features carrying these tests are enabled by their consumers (`bin/dwowd` enables `pow`). *The root-cause fix exists and is deliberately not taken*: `default = ["pow"]` would make the ad-hoc command complete, but a default feature propagates to every consumer, changing the dependency graph and the build of crates with no interest in PoW — a decision about the crate's feature layout rather than a verification row's to make, so it is recorded as the named option in the declarations file's header where the next reader will meet it. *Status `OPEN` → `PARTLY`*: the mechanisms are enumerated on every run, a new hidden test fails a gate rather than quietly reducing what a pass count means, and a *stale* declaration is reported rather than left to look like coverage — that is the half a gate can enforce. The convention itself — a claim names its command and its feature set — remains a rule a reader follows, and this row is where it is stated. `make test` was never affected |

**Fixed on 2026-09-22, in the rows above.** `OBL-C71` — `DataItem::deserialize` now rejects a declared
tag area that does not fit the buffer (`checked_add`, so the near-`u64::MAX` case cannot overflow), and
the test that used to assert the panic is the regression control asserting rejection.
`OBL-C69` — `PowSource` gained a codec of its own (`src/linear/src/serial_sync.rs`) and `Serialize`/
`Deserialize` impls that carry *its* bytes, so serde and the canonical codec cannot disagree about the
field; the `skip` is gone and the fail-open `_ => PowSource::Native` arm is now an error.
`OBL-C70` — follows from `OBL-C69`: the acceptance measure now grows with the merge-mining proof.
`OBL-C68` — the Arweave POST in `stratum_submit` is made with `linear_submit_lock` released; the
behavioural test is the Stage 4 integration test the row names, since the lock scope is not observable
without a stub Turbo server. `OBL-C76` — recorded, not fixed: it describes a verification *practice*.

**Landed 2026-09-22: the anchor proof rides in the block, and enforcement verifies it.** This is the
consensus-format change the design constraint above requires, and it **moves the header hash** — the
mining blob grew 260 → 292 bytes for `anchor_owner`, and the genesis pin was re-recorded from
`e281afa8…` to `538b1634…` as a declared consequence, not a masked one.

- **`OBL-C63` — CLOSED.** Both enforcement sites (`chain_state.rs:1011`, `:1546`) now require
  `caribina::verify_anchor_proof(&header)`, a pure local function. A block whose anchor cannot be
  verified confers **no** finality, and — deliberately — is not rejected outright either: rejecting
  would let any peer halt the chain with a malformed relay, whereas ignoring the claim costs only that
  block's finality. That asymmetry is what removes the free chain-freeze. The old predicate read
  `anchor_tx_id != 0 || anchor_monero_height != 0`, two fields a relaying peer chooses.
- **`OBL-C64` — CLOSED for the field that matters.** `anchor_owner` (a fresh per-block Ed25519 key)
  is inside the mining blob, so a peer cannot re-attribute an anchor to a block it did not mine
  without redoing the proof-of-work. The four post-mining fields remain outside the preimage — they
  must, or anchoring would invalidate the solution it anchors — and they are no longer consulted for
  finality, so their malleability buys an attacker nothing.
- **`OBL-C65` — CLOSED by construction, and the wrong invariant deleted.** `should_verify_anchor` and
  `should_verify_monero_anchor` had no production caller and returned `false` whenever their
  `*_enabled` flag was off while `should_enforce` ignored those flags — a node could enforce what it
  had decided not to check. They are **removed** rather than corrected: a dead method encoding a wrong
  invariant is worse than no method, and "a verifier with no caller" is this campaign's root cause.
  Enforcement and verification are now literally the same call.
- **`OBL-C66` — recorded here as still open; superseded later the same day, and now
  ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON.** An anchor is *verified*; Arweave *settlement*
  is not checked at all. It cannot be a consensus rule — settlement lives on another chain and
  consensus must be a pure function of local data — so what remains is the node-local settle policy
  the row's fix note describes. Recorded as open rather than quietly implied to be done.
  **Correction (2026-09-22, after this paragraph was written):** that "node-local settle policy" is
  **not** written, and the row does not wait on one. What the proof establishes is **publication** —
  the miner committed a key in the block's mined region, signed a DataItem binding that block, and
  published it — and publication is the property the 51% argument uses, because the adversary in scope
  cannot delete a DataItem. Settlement depth is Arweave's business and this chain does not read it, so
  the gap between *settled* and *published* is **recorded in the row rather than narrowed**, and
  `caribina.md` no longer advertises a settlement time. The paragraph above is kept as the record of
  what was believed before that decision, not as a task.
- **`OBL-C67` — still open.** The Monero anchor is not yet derived from `MoneroPowData`, and the local
  Monero PoW check is not yet in place.
- **Anchoring is now real on both miner paths.** The RPC miner anchored in a detached task that never
  mutated the header, so every block it committed carried zeroed anchor fields; the built-in `miner_task`
  never anchored at all. Both now build the proof after the nonce and attach it **before**
  `accept_block`, and publish it asynchronously afterwards — publication is best-effort and, crucially,
  no longer the thing finality depends on. Merge-mined blocks commit no `anchor_owner`: they have no
  DarkWow preimage of their own to bind one into, so Caribina is unavailable to that path by
  construction rather than by omission.
- **Tests.** `verify_anchor_proof` carries a positive control plus eight negatives, one per condition
  (no proof, zero owner, foreign signer, another block's proof, altered height, altered timestamp,
  truncated bytes, and a `tag_bytes` overflow); `anchor_commitment` is asserted invariant under the
  post-mining fields and sensitive to the owner and nonce; `chain_state` carries a verified-anchor
  positive control beside a forged-key control, with the unanchored case as the baseline. The
  `OBL-C63` and `OBL-C64` characterization tests are flipped, and two blob-length assertions moved.

**A design constraint derived while fixing the above, recorded because it is not obvious and it
determines the shape of the remaining change.** The tempting cheap fix for `OBL-C63`/`OBL-C64` is to
require only that a *descendant* commit the block's `anchor_tx_id` in its mined region — no proof in
the block, no `anchor_owner`, nothing verified at the enforcement site, and the anchor becomes
PoW-authenticated transitively by the next block's work. **That fix is unsound, and not marginally.**
`anchor_tx_id` would then be 32 bytes the miner chooses, so any miner could finalize any block they
mined by asserting an id and committing it in the next block; after two blocks every block would be
un-replaceable and the chain could never reorganise at all. A finality rule needs an artefact the
claimer cannot produce unilaterally, or it degrades into "no reorgs, ever". So the anchor **proof** —
the signed ANS-104 DataItem, authored by a key committed in the block's mined region and binding the
anchor-zeroed header hash — must ride inside the block and be verified by a pure local function. That
is why the remaining work is a consensus-format change rather than a predicate tweak, and why it must
be verified before it lands: it moves the header hash.

**One further defect, recorded but not opened as a finality row.** `mm_get_aux_block` parses p2pool's
`address` parameter, logs a warning and ignores it (`mm_rpc.rs:178-190`), so the node mines to its own
declared key and a merge miner's DarkWow reward does not route to the address it asked for. That is a
reward-routing and economic defect, not a finality one, and folding it into this residue would bury it
under the wrong root cause; it wants its own row and its own plan. Until then it is pinned by a test so
it cannot drift silently.

**Process items, not obligations.** From the same corpus, and they belong with the three the register
already carries: `compile-fragilities` `A4`/`A5` (`-j 8` and `--test-threads 4` for heavy sweeps —
both are standing rules in the repository's memory, not open findings); `A6` (`--no-fail-fast` on
sweeps, and the `aws-lc-rs`/`ring` `--all-features` conflict that makes `quic_transport` abort a
sweep); `build-resource` `B7` (BuildKit, not configured — a deliberate future enhancement); and the
`hazop-darkleaf-in-contractcall-data` proposal, whose own verdict was **REJECT** and which was never
implemented — recorded as closed-by-rejection rather than left to look like untracked work.

### The contract phase rules — found by reading the genesis contracts as a benchmark (`OBL-C72`+)

The nine genesis contracts were read end to end, not sampled, and set against the other 25. They
observe two rules **without exception**, and three of them state the rule in a comment:
`native_token/src/entrypoint/mod.rs:1405-1407` ("Exec computed the running total; Apply only writes
it — no read in Apply — db_get ACL excludes Update"), `box/src/entrypoint/mod.rs:143-144`, and
`purse/src/entrypoint/mod.rs:202`. Both rules are normative in
[contract-wasm-type-system.md](../arch/contract-wasm-type-system.md) §A.4.7 and §B.2.2 and enforced
mechanically by the host ACL. **Neither has a register row, a checklist line, or a gate script** —
`grep -rln "ContractSection\|acl_allow\|read triad" scripts/ script/*.py hooks/` returns nothing, and
no script in the repository references the ACL section model at all.

That absence is the finding, and it is what makes these rows worth separating from `OBL-C1`–`C71`.
Every other rule in this repository that *has* a gate — domain separation, instance derivation,
metadata alignment, pubkey binding — appears in this register as measured and is wholly or partly
closed. These two had none, and drifted to **86 violations across 12 contracts, none of them genesis**.

The measurement is `scripts/check-phase-host-functions.sh`, which parses each contract's declared
`define_contract!` entrypoints, builds the call closure from `exec` and `apply`, and takes the
permitted sections from the `acl_allow` call sites in `src/runtime/import/*.rs` **at run time** — so
the gate cannot drift from the ACL it checks, the RC5 failure this repository has had four times.
**The gate corrected this row's first draft**: an earlier ad-hoc scan reported 66 sites and missed
`insurance_market` entirely (15 sites — its apply handlers are `*_process_update_v1` in per-function
files, which a name-pattern scan does not match) and the read-only getters `get_verifying_block_height`
(four sites) and `get_call_index` (one), whose ACLs also exclude `Update`. The 86 is the gate's
number; 66 was a pattern-matching artifact.

**Re-measured 2026-09-23, and both of those numbers are now stale in the same direction.** The gate
reports **7 violations, in one contract**, all one class:

```
$ bash scripts/check-phase-host-functions.sh ; echo EXIT=$?
FAIL: src/contract/drain_protection/src/entrypoint.rs:368: init_fund_process_instruction_v1() -> db_set()  (legal in ('Deploy', 'Update'))
       … (the same shape at :439, :491, :542, :600, :630, :662 — seven sites, all drain_protection)
FAIL: 7 phase violation(s).
EXIT=1
```

So **read-in-apply is at 0 sites everywhere** — `OBL-C72`'s rule now holds without exception — and
**write-in-exec is at 7, all in `drain_protection`**. Two corrections follow from the same run: the
**86** above and the *script's own docstring* (`check-phase-host-functions.sh:20`, "66 sites across
11 non-genesis contracts") are both stale self-descriptions of a family that has moved; and the
seven survivors are the residue of a contract whose state cannot commit at all — its `process_update`
is a no-op (`drain_protection/src/entrypoint.rs:791-794`, `Ok(())` with the update discarded), so the
exec-phase `db_set` is the *only* write path and every transition fails the ACL. That is `OBL-C73`'s
proposition measured false, and it is recorded there rather than here.

**And the gate that found this is not wired into `run-all-tests.sh`.** Its `run_gate` list (lines
76-146) has no `check-phase-host-functions` entry, so seven live violations cannot fail a full run —
which is the same "a gate nobody runs" shape this family's own filing text names, one level up.

**Corrected 2026-09-23: it is wired, as a ratchet.** `run-all-tests.sh` now runs
`exec/apply phase rule` with the static audits, and `scripts/check-phase-host-functions.sh` takes
its seven known sites from `script/phase_host_function_exceptions.txt` (content-keyed on
`<file> : <fn>() -> <host>()` rather than on line numbers, following
`script/circuit_free_instances.txt`), printing each as `EXCEPTED` with the register ID that
schedules it and exiting 0 while a *new* violation still exits 1. Negative-controlled both ways
before wiring: with the exception file removed the gate exits 1 with all seven, and with one entry
dropped it exits 1 naming exactly that site — so the exceptions are demonstrably what suppresses
the findings rather than a bug in the filter. The paragraph above is kept as the record of what
was true when the row was filed; what it named as the gap is now the mechanism that closes it.

| ID | Status | Proposition | Source | Sev |
|---|---|---|---|
| OBL-C72 | **SATISFIED** | The `apply` entrypoint performs blind writes only: no `db_get`, `db_contains_key`, `get_object_size` or `get_object_bytes` is reachable from it, and any value apply needs is computed in `exec` and carried through the update struct | §A.4.7, §B.2.2; `src/runtime/import/db.rs:638`,`:787`,`:1325`,`:1551`; `vm_runtime.rs:954` | M |
| OBL-C73 | **CLOSED** | A state write (`db_set`, `db_del`, `merkle_add`, `sparse_merkle_insert_batch`) is not reachable from an `exec`-phase function; all mutation is in `apply` | §A.4.7, §B.2.2; `src/runtime/import/db.rs:358`,`:504`; `merkle.rs:50`; `smt.rs:142` | M | **Measured 2026-09-23, and the row is the whole of the class: seven sites, one contract, one shape.** `scripts/check-phase-host-functions.sh` reports exactly seven violations, all `drain_protection`, all `() -> db_set()`, and the register's own earlier note names the same seven lines (`entrypoint.rs:368`, `:439`, `:491`, `:542`, `:600`, `:630`, `:662`). *What they mean.* `vm_runtime.rs:954` runs apply as `ContractSection::Update`, the host returns `CALLER_ACCESS_DENIED` when the section is outside a function's `acl_allow` list, and **no write function admits `Exec` at all** — so each of the seven is a call that cannot succeed, not a slow path. *And the other half, which is worse than the plan's note recorded.* This contract's `process_update` is a no-op: `fn process_update(_cid, _update_data)` (`:791-792`) ignores both arguments and only logs, so even the apply phase writes nothing. The `encode_*_update_v1` helpers (`:711-775`) build update bytes that nothing consumes. So the contract's state is never written *anywhere*, which is why its heavyweight coverage cannot be green and why the seven are scheduled rather than merely tolerated. *The gate is now wired*, as a ratchet over exactly these seven with the reason per site in `script/phase_host_function_exceptions.txt` — see the paragraph above on this page for the negative controls, and `run-all-tests.sh` now runs it with the static audits. *The repair*, in this row's own terms and first in the contract-rework band: move each write into the corresponding apply function, carry the computed value through the update struct, and give `process_update` a body that decodes and writes rather than a signature that discards its argument. When that lands, the seven lines come out of the exception list in the same commit, and the gate's exception count goes from seven to zero without the gate itself changing. **CLOSED 2026-09-23, and the repair turned out to be a contract rework rather than a move — which is the finding worth recording, because the row expected the latter.** Each of the seven writes is now in an apply function beside a `process_update` that had been a no-op; but five of the seven **could not be moved as they stood**, because their update structs carried identifiers and nothing else. `InitializeUpdateV1` held `{instance_seed, fund_id}` while the write stores the whole `ProtectedFund`; the comment in the entrypoint admitted it ("Store fund directly (InitializeUpdateV1 only has fund_id)"). Apply may neither read nor validate (`§B.2.2`), so the value has to travel in the update: **the updates now carry what they write** — the fund for initialize/lock/unlock/update_config, the key and value for vote, the key and record for transfer — with the scalar fields that were exactly `fund.<field>` folded into the fund rather than copied beside it. The encoders gained a length prefix for the variable-length payloads, following `ProposeParamsV1`'s proof in the same file. The shape is not invented: `identity`'s entrypoint is the repository's worked example (`process_*_instruction` builds the update, `apply_*_update` writes it, `process_update` dispatches on the selector byte), and that is what this now mirrors. *Verified on its own terms*: `scripts/check-phase-host-functions.sh` → **PASS: no unexcepted phase violations**, with the seven entries gone from `script/phase_host_function_exceptions.txt` — which is now **empty**, emptied by repairing the defect rather than by widening the list. `cargo test -p dwow_drain_protection_contract --all-features` → **24 passed, 0 failed**, including the four rewritten update round-trips that now assert the payload apply writes survives the length-prefixed encoding. The artifact rebuilt; `check-artifact-freshness.sh` green. *And one thing this row had wrong*: it said the missing writes are why the heavyweight coverage cannot be green. They are **a** reason, but not the first — the test fails at its first endpoint inside the *harness*, `Proof::create failed`, because `drain_protection_spec.rs` runs every endpoint against placeholder proofs ("Client proof modules pending"). That is its own finding, filed as `OBL-C88`, and it is why this repair is verified by the gate and the unit round-trips rather than end to end. *The gate also gained the check that proves the repair complete*: an exception whose site no longer matches is reported as stale, so the seven entries announced their own removal in one run instead of being pruned by hand — before that, a stale entry would have gone on admitting the next finding of the same shape |
| OBL-C74 | **PARTLY** | Every contract declares in its manifest the barbs each action requires, the capabilities it defines, its note schema and its capability primitives — the declaration `type-system.md` §13 and `ocap.md` §7 make the basis of `wallet_construct` | `manifest.md`; `ocap.md` §7; `contract-wasm-type-system.md` §A.2.2, §A.0.3, §13 | M |
| OBL-C75 | **OPEN** | The id a host uses to key state is the id its circuit derives and `get_metadata` publishes — the host does not independently recompute it from data the client cannot know | `safety.md` RC5 (one fact, two sources of truth); `contract-wasm-type-system.md` §A.1.6, §A.2 | M | **First instance measured 2026-09-23, in `subscription`, and it fails on both halves.** The row had no evidence recorded; this is what the code says. *The circuit does derive the id.* `SubscribeV2` computes `derived_id = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_COMMITMENT, subscriber_pub_x, subscriber_pub_y, plan_id, deposit, asset_id, lock_until_block, subscriber_secret, nonce)` (`subscribe.zk:42-51`) and compares it against the witness `subscription_id` (`:52`) — an equality whose operands are both prover-chosen, since the circuit's only `constrain_instance` targets are `tx_binding` and `tx_nonce` (`:60-62`). *The metadata does not publish it.* The SubscribeV1 arm of `get_metadata` publishes `vec![pallas::Base::zero(), pallas::Base::zero()]` under the comment `// SubscribeV2 circuit: [tx_binding, tx_nonce]` (`entrypoint.rs:149-155`), so the derived id reaches no verifier — and, noted here because the two repairs interact, the zeros are `OBL-C78`'s and `OBL-Z2`'s defect: taken literally they make the arm's proof unverifiable, since the circuit instances `poseidon_hash(3, tx_commitment, tx_nonce)` and `tx_nonce`. *The host keys state on the client's value.* `subscribe_v1` builds the record with `id: params.commitment` (`:358-360`) and `subscribe_apply_v1` writes it with a blind `db_set` under `params.commitment.to_bytes()` (`:392-396`); the `db_mark_spent` beside it is itself `db_set(db, key, &[1])` (`sdk/src/wasm/db.rs:185-187`), so nothing checks whether the key already exists. *The consequence, as far as it was measured.* A second `SubscribeV1` carrying a `commitment` already present overwrites that record. It does **not** lock the first subscriber out of `update_usage`: `Subscription::compute_nullifier` is `poseidon_hash([self.id.inner(), secret])` (`model/mod.rs:415-417`), the id is unchanged by an overwrite, and the secret is the caller's, so the victim's own nullifier still matches (`entrypoint.rs:627-631`). What changes is the record those checks then act on — plan, duration, `uses_allowed`, `lock_until_block` are the attacker's, so this is a downgrade the attacker pays the plan's price for (the host enforces it from the stored plan, `:349-353`), not a theft. That cost is why this row stays **M**. *The repair is two-sided for the reason the row's two halves are*: publish the derived id in `get_metadata` (which is `OBL-C78`'s mechanism, so the two land together) **and** have the host key on the published id rather than on `params.commitment` — publishing alone leaves the collision, and fixing the key alone leaves the value a verifier would compare unpublished |
| OBL-C77 | **CLOSED** | A metadata arm for a callable non-ZK function returns an **encoded** empty public-input vector, not a bare empty `Vec` | `execution.rs:423`; `native_token/src/entrypoint/mod.rs:922` (`plaintext_call_get_metadata`, the reference); contract-standards.md §3 | M | **The mechanism, on both sides, before any site: a bare empty buffer is not "no public inputs" to the host — it is the documented rejection signal.** `execution.rs` decodes the first encoded vector out of `metadata()` and, on an empty buffer, sets `fail_stage = "metadata-decode-zkp"` and fails the call, *before exec runs*. So an arm whose success path returns `vec![]` makes its instruction impossible to call, and the reason never appears in the contract's own log. The reference for the correct shape is `native_token`'s `plaintext_call_get_metadata`, which returns `zk_public_inputs.encode()` (an encoded empty vector) on success and the empty buffer only for its two rejection cases. **Measured, not inherited from the plan's line numbers** — those were stale, and the class is 12 arms across 11 contracts. Two of them were live defects, and they were in one contract: `relayer_endowment`'s `get_metadata` had explicit arms for three functions and `_ => vec![]` for **eight** more — WithdrawDeployment, SettleFees, UpdateConfig, ForceSettle, DeactivateEndowment and the three `relayer`-gated ones — with `// No ZK circuits for WithdrawDeployment, SettleFees, UpdateConfig` sitting directly above it. No circuits is exactly why they must answer with the *encoded empty* vector, and instead every one of the eight was rejected at the metadata stage: eight callable instructions that could never succeed, none of them ever exercised. They now each answer through a ported copy of the reference helper, with their params decoded first so a malformed call still gets the rejection signal, and the three gated arms carry the same `#[cfg(feature = "relayer")]` as their exec arms so the two dispatches stay in step. **The other ten are declared, not repaired, and the declaration is the review**: nine are `Initialize`/`InitializeV1` — the deploy-time instruction, whose exec arm either says so in words ("has no instruction data" / "has no update data") or returns `Err(InvalidFunction)`, and whose state is created by `init_contract` rather than by a call — and one is `bearer_bond`'s `VerifyCoverageV1`, whose exec function is a documented stub carrying the FIXME for the sentinel-key lookup it needs. For those ten the empty answer **is** the intended refusal; what was missing is that a reader could not tell that from an encode that had been forgotten. **A gate, because a shape check is the honest form of this rule**: `scripts/check-metadata-arms.sh` finds every `get_metadata` arm whose value is a literal empty vector, requires each to be declared in `script/metadata_plaintext_exceptions.txt` with the reason, and reports a stale entry — negative-controlled both ways (one entry dropped → exit 1 naming exactly that site; a fake entry → reported stale, run still passes). It is wired into `run-all-tests.sh`, and it is deliberately shallow: an arm returning a *variable* is out of scope, because a shape check that tried to follow values is the cleverer classifier this register has twice rejected. **Found while doing this, and fixed in the same commit**: the `relayer` feature configuration **did not compile at all** — `src/relayer.rs:258` used `SerializedLen` without importing it — so the three gated instructions had never been built in any configuration, and my new arms for them could not be verified until it did. One import line; all three configurations (default, `--features relayer`, `--all-features`) now compile. **Not verified end to end, and the reason is `OBL-C88`**: `test_heavyweight_relayer_endowment` still fails at its *first* endpoint with `invalid proof: call[0] namespace 'InitializeV2'` — a proof that does not verify against the circuit it names, which is the harness's problem and not this row's, and it is why the eight now-repaired arms are covered by the gate rather than by that test |
| OBL-C78 | **FAILS** | A contract's `get_metadata` publishes, for each circuit, a public-input vector whose **count and order** agree with the circuit's `constrain_instance` list; the tx pair is `poseidon_hash([3, tx_commitment, tx_nonce])` and is never a literal zero | `pool_stake/src/client/create_pool.rs:84-90` (the worked template); `src/sdk/src/crypto/constants.rs:57`; `check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh`; `privacy.md` §5.3 | M | **Measured 2026-09-23, and the rule is now enforced rather than asserted.** `check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh` gained a hard literal-vs-value rule (see `OBL-C20`): a position where the circuit instances a value and the metadata pushes a literal `Base::zero()` is a **finding**, not an advisory warning, because a literal can only match a derived value by a preimage coincidence and cannot match a client-supplied witness either. The single reviewed exemption is `tx_nonce`, left zero by convention across 71 positions; the exemption list is in the script, so adding to it is a deliberate act. *The sites — 10 positions over 6 circuits, with **zero overlap** against the 19 circuits the count check already fails, so this is a class that was invisible rather than one restated:* `drain_protection/exit` instance 1 (`tx_binding`); `stablecoin/init` instance 1 (`tx_binding`); `subscription/subscribe` instance 1 (`tx_binding`); `subscription/update_usage` instance 1 (`tx_binding`); and `subscription/cancel` and `subscription/renew`, each with three — instances 1, 2 and 3, namely `subscription_id`, `spent_nullifier` and `tx_binding`. *The composition differs from the one this work was planned against and adds to it*: the plan named `dao_escrow`, `drain_protection`, `pool_stake` and `subscription`; the measurement confirms two of those, **adds `stablecoin/init`**, and does not report `dao_escrow` or `pool_stake` — not because they are clean, but because both already fail on *counts* and the order comparison does not run against a push shorter than the instance list. That is a limitation stated in the script, and the honest reading of their absence is "not measured", not "clear". *Why the `subscription` four carry the row's proposition hardest*: `cancel` and `renew` push four zeros for a vector whose circuit instances `subscription_id`, `spent_nullifier`, `tx_binding` and `tx_nonce` (`entrypoint.rs:180-193`), and `subscribe`'s arm pushes `vec![zero, zero]` under the comment `// SubscribeV2 circuit: [tx_binding, tx_nonce]` (`:149-155`) — a comment naming the pair it does not derive, which is the shape this register treats as a claim contradicted by its own code. *The repair is this row's own*: publish `poseidon_hash([3, tx_commitment, tx_nonce])` and the real `tx_nonce`, and publish the values the remaining positions instance. Neither stablecoin nor subscription is genesis, so none of it moves the pin — but a repaired arm must also be matched by its **client**, which is what makes this larger than a metadata-only edit. **Measured again 2026-09-23 while working Band 5, and the size is an order of magnitude above this row's own estimate: the *count* half is the live defect, 19 circuits over 6 contracts, and the worst of it is one contract's eight arms publishing two literal zeros each.** The gate's worklist, read as a worklist rather than as a summary: `drain_protection` **8 circuits** (`5 constrain_instance vs 2 pushed values`); `insurance_market` **4** (`5/6/5/5` vs 3); `tender` **5** (`create_tender` pushes nothing at all for its namespace, `reveal_bid` 7 vs 5, `select_winner` 6 vs 4, `submit_bid` 6 vs 4, `submit_bid_with_capability` 8 vs 6); `lottery/initialize` and `subscription/verify_access` push **nothing** for their namespaces. And the shape in `drain_protection` is not a count slip — every one of its eight ZK arms pushes `vec![pallas::Base::zero(), pallas::Base::zero()]` (`entrypoint.rs:207`, `:216`, …), two literal zeros, under the circuit's real five. So the count is wrong *and* the values are placeholders, and the host compares the published vector against the proof's actual instance vector: these instructions could never verify. **Why that was invisible, and it is `OBL-C88` again**: `drain_protection`'s heavyweight test dies at its first endpoint on `Proof::create failed`, so its metadata has never been checked against a real proof by anything — the harness cannot build one and the metadata cannot describe one, and each defect hides the other. **What the repair requires per circuit, measured on one example**: `lock.zk` instances `authority_pub_x, authority_pub_y, authority_nullifier, tx_binding, tx_nonce`; the arm publishes two zeros. `authority_pub_x/y` are derivable from state (`fund.spend_authority` is stored and the witness is its secret), but `authority_nullifier = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_NULLIFIER, fund_id, authority_secret)` **is not** — it needs a value only the client has, so it must be carried in the *params*. That is the same `C73` shape one level over: the params must carry what the metadata publishes, which means a wire-format change per instruction *and* the matching client change, across 19 circuits. **This is therefore a campaign with the gate as its worklist, not a band item**, and it is recorded as such here rather than started at the end of a long one: the ordering inside it is by contract (`drain_protection` first, both because its eight are uniform and because repairing them is what would let `C88`'s fixture work be checked against something). **`tender` taken first instead, 2026-09-23, and the reason is what the first pass found: it is the one contract in the list whose *client* exists.** Its five circuits each instance a `tx_binding`/`tx_nonce` pair the metadata omitted, so the fix is the three-sided one the row describes — and all three sides were wrong, which is the finding. (i) The **params** carried no tx pair at all, so the metadata had nothing to publish: the pair is now appended to all five (`CreateTender`, `SubmitBid`, `RevealBid`, `SelectWinner`, `SubmitBidWithCapability`), encoded after the last existing field so every prior position is unchanged and an old-format call is refused by the min-length guard rather than decoded shifted. (ii) The **metadata** published 2/5/4/4/6 values under circuits that instance 4/7/6/6/8 — the pair missing from each, with the bidder's or requester's coordinates already read from stored state. (iii) The **client** instanced `tx_binding: pallas::Base::zero()` — a literal, in all five proof builders — which no circuit derives for any input, so every tender proof failed verification against its own circuit. One derivation now feeds all of it: `client::tx_binding_of(tx_commitment, tx_nonce)` = `poseidon_hash([DRK_POSEIDON_DOMAIN_TX_BINDING, …])`, used by the five clients, the five params builders (whose `tx_pair` setter is the way a caller binds a real transaction) and the harness fixtures. **The gate had been calling this a different defect and only half of it**: `tender/create_tender` was reported as "no metadata push carries TENDER_CONTRACT_ZKAS_CREATE_TENDER_NS_V2" while its arm pushed under `TENDER_CONTRACT_ZKAS_CREATE_NS_V2` — the same namespace string, an alias pair — so the gate's value→name table kept one name and missed the other. It now maps a value to *every* constant carrying it, which also cleans `subscription`'s `VERIFY_ACCESS`/`VERIFY` pair. **Result: the count half is 19 → 13, and all five tender circuits read `OK: N constrain_instance, N metadata pushes, N client public inputs`** — the gate's strongest form, which checks the client's `to_vec` against the circuit as well. `cargo test -p dwow_tender_contract` → 25 passed, 0 failed, including two length controls updated by exactly the 64 bytes the pair adds. **And the heavyweight test still fails at the same endpoint, which is what the localisation is for.** `test_heavyweight_tender` reports `invalid proof: call[0] namespace 'CreateTenderV2'` before and after — so a new test was written to answer *where*: `test-harness/tests/tender_create_proof.rs` builds the client's proof and verifies it against **the same zkbin the contract embeds**, with the inputs `to_vec()` produces, never touching the host. It fails: `InvalidProof`, with the zero pair **and** with a non-zero one. So the contract's metadata is no longer the reason — the client's proof does not verify against its own circuit with its own public inputs, and the defect is below this row's layer (the witnesses, the circuit, or the fixtures). That is the next pass's subject and it now has a red test naming it rather than a green gate hiding it. `tender` is not genesis, so all of this is pin-neutral **The literal class re-read 2026-09-23, three sides per circuit, and it is 11 positions over 7 circuits — one circuit more than this row recorded.** `subscription/verify_access` instance 1 joins the list, so the composition is this row's ten plus that one. Re-run rather than recalled: `bash scripts/check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh` → *"11 position(s) push a literal `Base::zero()` where the circuit instances a value"* over `drain_protection/exit`, `stablecoin/init`, `subscription/{subscribe,update_usage,verify_access}` and `subscription/{cancel,renew}` (three each). *The class confirmed on all three sides for the new member, and the confirmation is what makes it a finding rather than a count.* The circuit **derives** the value it exposes — `tx_binding = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_TX_BINDING, tx_commitment, tx_nonce)` then `constrain_instance(tx_binding)` (`verify_access.zk:44-45`) — while the host's metadata arm answers with `vec![pallas::Base::zero(), pallas::Base::zero()]` under the comment `// VerifyAccessV2 circuit: [tx_binding, tx_nonce]` (`entrypoint.rs:157-161`), and the runtime checks the proof *against that vector* (`src/linear/src/zk_verifier.rs:234-238`). The client's half is the third side and it does not rescue it: `client/verify_access.rs:157` puts `tx_binding: pallas::Base::zero()` into the public inputs and `:179` puts zero in the witness too, while the circuit overwrites the witness with the derived hash. So the instance column would have to hold zero *and* satisfy `instance = poseidon(3, tx_commitment, tx_nonce)` — satisfiable only by a preimage of zero — and the same read reproduces for `subscribe`, `update_usage`, `cancel`, `renew`, `stablecoin/init` and `drain_protection/exit`: every one **derives** the binding, every arm answers with a literal, so none of those seven instructions can be called successfully by anyone. That is the `OBL-C77`/`OBL-C90` class (an instruction with no path that works) reached through the binding rather than the declaration, and it is why the repair is this row's own across all seven rather than a metadata-only edit: the *client* must build the same derivation, which is the shape `tender`'s landed fix already has and the reason `pool_stake`'s client is the worked template above **The `subscription` arm repaired 2026-09-23 on all three sides, and the literal class is down from 11 positions over 7 circuits to 2 over 2.** *What was wrong, read rather than inherited from the gate's summary.* Five circuits instance the pair — `tx_binding = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_TX_BINDING, tx_commitment, tx_nonce)`, then `tx_nonce` — and five arms published literals in their place: `subscribe` and `verify_access` two zeros each; `update_usage` two zeros beside a correctly derived `derived_id`; and `cancel`/`renew` **four** zeros each, including `subscription_id` and `spent_nullifier`, which the params already carried. No satisfiable proof can put a derived value into a literal instance slot, so all five instructions were unbuildable. *The repair, in this row's own shape.* The pair joined the params of all five — `SubscribeParamsV1`, `UpdateUsageParamsV1`, `VerifyAccessParamsV1`, `CancelParamsV1`, `RenewParamsV1` — appended after each struct's last existing field, so every prior position is unchanged and an old-format call is refused by the length guard rather than decoded shifted; the three clients that exist derive it through one helper (`client::tx_binding_of`, copied from `tender/src/client/mod.rs:59` rather than re-derived) and expose `tx_pair`; the arms publish the params' values in the circuit's order. *Two things the read turned up beyond the literals.* `update_usage`'s client `to_vec` was in a **different order** from its circuit — `[derived_id, tx_binding, tx_nonce]` against the circuit's `[tx_binding, tx_nonce, derived_id]` — which no count check can see and no proof survives; it is fixed here. And `cancel`/`renew` have **no client at all**: `src/contract/subscription/src/client/` holds `subscribe`, `update_usage`, `verify_access`, `rate_limit` and `zkbins`, so their pair is the zero default the harness derives and their proofs stay unbuildable until a client is written. That is recorded rather than papered over, and it is the remaining half of those two instructions. *Verified.* Gate 2 reads the strongest form for the three with clients — `subscription/subscribe` 2 constrain_instance, 2 pushes, 2 client public inputs; `update_usage` 3/3/3; `verify_access` 2/2/2 — and count-agreement for the two without (`cancel` 4/4, `renew` 4/4, `no client to_vec found`); the instance checker classifies all five with nothing unclassified (`subscribe 2 derived:1, redundant:1`; `cancel`/`renew` `4 bound:1, derived:1, redundant:2`); the contract's own 27 tests pass, including the round-trips that now exercise the appended pair; the artifact was rebuilt and is fresh, and this contract is not genesis so nothing moved the pin. *And the end-to-end run says where the next blocker is, which is not here.* `test_heavyweight_subscription` still fails, but **not at the proof**: `grep -c "invalid proof"` over the run's log is **0**, where this row's symptom was `invalid proof: call[0] namespace …`, and the failure is now the contract's own child-call check — *"Expected 1 child call (promissory_note::transfer_v1), got 0"*, `ContractError(Custom(30))`. The spec's five endpoints all declare `children: vec![]` while `subscribe_v1` requires the payment child, so the fixture lacks it; that is a test-side gap this row does not own and it is named as the next step rather than closed from here. *Severity unchanged at `M`*: five instructions move from unbuildable to buildable, two stay unbuildable for want of a client, and gate 2's remaining literal findings are `drain_protection/exit` and `stablecoin/init` — the former this plan's Band 3 cluster, the latter the same shape in a contract already rebuilt once today **And `stablecoin/init` fixed 2026-09-23 by the same recipe, which leaves the literal class at 1 position over 1 circuit.** `init.zk` instances `tx_binding`, `tx_nonce` and `deployer_auth`; the arm published `[zero, zero, params.deployer_auth]`. What the read found is that **the client was already right** — `client/initialize.rs`'s `InitV1CallData::tx_binding()` derives `poseidon_hash([3, tx_commitment, tx_nonce])` and `compute_public_inputs` uses it — so the literal was the only thing standing between a real init proof and the vector the host expected: client and host disagreed about a value neither could see, and the disagreement was invisible because no count differs when a zero is pushed. The pair joined `InitializeParams` after `governance_pub_y`, with the length guard moved 245 → 309 so an old-format call is refused rather than decoded shifted; the arm publishes the params' values; `InitV1CallData` gained `tx_pair`; and the two fixtures that build the params on the deploy path (`stablecoin_spec.rs`'s `deploy_ix`, `tests/pipeline.rs`) carry the zero pair's derived binding. Verified: gate 2 reads `stablecoin/init — 3 constrain_instance, 3 metadata pushes`; the contract's own 15 tests pass; `test_heavyweight_stablecoin` — which deploys through `deploy_ix` and is also `OBL-C82`'s control — passes at **563.64s**, so the deploy path and the accrual check are both exercised on the changed params. **What remains of this row's literal class is one position in one circuit: `drain_protection/exit`**, whose repair is not a literal edit but the client proof module that does not exist — `OBL-C88`'s cluster, and Band 3's largest item. **And the subscription fixture's gap is wider than the child calls, read out so the next pass does not start from the wrong end.** The spec creates no **plan** either, and `subscribe_v1` looks one up and rejects an unknown id (`:379-387`), so the fixture needs a `DaoControlV1::UpdatePlan` first — that is the action which stores a plan (`:795-800`, and the arm's own comment records that it once persisted nothing while exec wrote and apply's arm said "nothing to do here") — then notes issued through the PN harness, and only then a child whose value commit satisfies `validate_child_value_commit(child, plan.price, poseidon_hash([plan.price, params.commitment.inner()]))` (`:396-400`). *One claim I nearly recorded and did not*: that no path creates a plan at all. `grep` for a `plans_db` write finds one at `:857` inside `dao_control_apply_v1`, and reading `:795-800` shows it is a creation rather than a toggle — so the plan half has a mechanism and the fixture simply does not use it. The host half of this row is done; the fixture is a flow, not a patch |
| OBL-C79 | **SATISFIED** | A checker enumerates the **whole class** it claims to check, reports the scope it did not examine, and reconciles the set it walked against the set it declared | `check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh:71` (`GENESIS`, the counter-example); the four gates that already glob (`check-circuit-domain-separation.sh:27`, `check-pubkey-binding.sh:44`, `check-artifact-freshness.sh:67`, `check-phase-host-functions.sh:175`) | M |

| OBL-C81 | **OPEN** | A capability check authorizes the holder it names, only for the issued capability — the purse's own instance of it: the ownership a purse **records** is the ownership its withdraw **requires**, so `owner_commit` is compared against something | `scripts/check-pubkey-binding.sh` — 3 of its 57 candidates, and the only shape in the genesis slice that does not resolve; `purse/proof/{balance,deposit,withdraw}.zk`; `purse/src/model/mod.rs:108` | M | **Found 2026-09-23 by adjudicating `OBL-Z18`'s genesis-contract candidates (see that row), and recorded here because it is a different proposition from the detector's.** The shape is `constrain_equal_base(derived_owner, owner_pub)`, where `derived_owner = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_SIGNATURE_SECRET, owner_secret)`. **The row said "all three purse circuits" and that is wrong for one of them, measured by reading both sides on 2026-09-23 — so the claim is corrected here rather than left, because a row that overstates its own extent is the thing this register exists not to be.** `balance.zk` is **not** an instance: `owner_pub` is consumed at `balance.zk:38` by `dp_circuit = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_COMMITMENT, owner_pub, asset_id, purse_id)`, constrained to the caller's `derived_purse_id` (`:39`) and exposed (`:40`), so the equality at `:44` transmits that exposure to `derived_owner` — the transitive arm the `escrow` slice earned, applied here. The exception-list entry that carried the false claim was reworded under this row. **The two that remain are `deposit.zk` and `withdraw.zk`, and for them every clause holds**: `owner_pub` appears only at its witness declaration and at the equality (`deposit.zk:32`, `:49`; `withdraw.zk:32`, `:50`), `derived_owner` is exposed nowhere, and the host contains the substring `owner` **zero** times in its 225 lines. **Two things found by re-reading, and one of them is worse than the row states.** (i) *The prescribed repair cannot be built as written.* The row's remedy is "expose `derived_owner` and have the withdraw path compare it against the stored `owner_commit`" — but there is no stored `owner_commit` to compare against. `Purse { version, purse_id, token_commit, balance_commit, owner_commit }` (`purse/src/model/mod.rs:108`) is constructed **only in a test** (`purse/tests/integration.rs:73`); `Purse {` appears nowhere else under the contract, the host never writes it to a tree and never reads it from one, and `model/mod.rs:106`'s own note says "When wire-format usage begins, this doc comment must be removed" — i.e. the record has never been wired. So one half of the pair the repair names does not exist, and the repair is "build the purse record's persistence, then bind it", not "compare against it". (ii) *The harm is stronger than the row's narrative*. The row says the merkle-leaf preimage is the only real gate, so a leak of it is what the missing check would backstop. Reading `withdraw.zk`'s nullifier: `nf_circuit = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_NULLIFIER, owner_secret, purse_id, state_nonce)` is exposed and **nothing compares it to any stored nullifier**, so `owner_secret` is not even constrained to a registered owner — it is free. Severity stays M on the same reasoning the row gives (the leaf preimage gates it, and no path from untrusted input to stolen funds is demonstrated), but the second lock is missing on both the ownership and the nullifier side | So the constraint binds one witness to a hash of another and states an ownership property the verifier never receives. What makes it a finding rather than a style point is that the *record* expects it: `Purse { version, purse_id, token_commit, balance_commit, owner_commit }` (`purse/src/model/mod.rs:108`) stores an `owner_commit` that **nothing in the tree ever compares against** — not the circuit, not the host. A half-built lock, and the half that is missing is the side that would check it. *Why this is M and not C or H, stated as the path that fails rather than as an assurance.* For a withdraw to be accepted the prover must reproduce `old_leaf` and a merkle path to the exposed `expected_root`, which requires `old_balance` and `state_nonce`. Both are private to the owner's wallet and appear on chain only inside a Pedersen commitment (`new_commit_x/y`) and a leaf hash, so an attacker without them cannot build the path — which means the search for a path from untrusted input to stolen funds fails *at the same gate the vacuous check would have failed at*. So there is no demonstrated harm, and this register does not rank an undemonstrated one. What the missing check costs is the second lock: **if the leaf preimage ever leaks — a wallet bug, a log, a backup — nothing else stands between the holder of those two field elements and the balance**, and the `owner_commit` that was supposed to be that lock is inert. Defence-in-depth, therefore M. *The repair*, when it is worked: expose `derived_owner` and have the withdraw path compare it against the stored `owner_commit` — the shape `Z17`'s remedy uses for identity's capability, and the same shape `labor_market` uses as the repository's one working capability check. **That repair is ordered, not available**: it presupposes a stored `owner_commit`, and per (i) above nothing persists the `Purse` record at all, so the work is "wire the purse record, then bind against it". Recorded so the next reader does not start from a comparison whose right-hand side does not exist |
| OBL-C82 | **PARTLY** | Interest accrued on the pool's debt is determined by the contract's configured rate and the elapsed time since the last accrual, and the accrual is authorized by the authority registered at init | `accrue_interest.zk:32-36`, `:49-67`; `stablecoin/src/entrypoint.rs:1528-1593`, `:1279`, `:1701`; `CDP_LAST_INTEREST_UPDATE_KEY` (`:100`, set only at `:203`), `CDP_BASE_RATE` (`lib.rs:84`, stored at `:128`), `STABLECOIN_CONTRACT_GOVERNANCE_PUBKEY_KEY` (read only at `:1381`) | M | **Found 2026-09-23 by adjudicating `OBL-Z18`'s `stablecoin` candidates, from the two `OBL-Z9`-shaped ones; the sweep of the two sides then produced the rest.** *The circuit.* `accumulator_public = ec_mul_base(accumulator_secret, NULLIFIER_K)` (`:32`) and `:35-36` compare its `ec_get_x/y` against the witnesses `accumulator_pub_x/y`. Neither operand is a `constrain_instance` target — the circuit's exposed set is `old_total_debt` (`:39`), `tx_binding` (`:70`) and `tx_nonce` (`:71`) — so the equality is between two prover-chosen values and holds for any secret. The comment above it, `# Verify accumulator is authorized`, names the property; the constraint states nothing. This is `OBL-Z9`'s shape verbatim — *"an equality between two prover-chosen values, holding for any secret"* — in a contract that row's scope does not list. *The host does not supply the missing check.* `process_accrue_interest_instruction` (`:1528`) reads the config DB only for `CDP_TOTAL_DEBT_KEY` (`:1548`) and `CDP_ACCUMULATED_FEES_KEY` (`:1576`), and never compares `params.accumulator_pub` against the point stored at init. That comparison exists two functions away, for governance reports (`:1381`, written at `:197`) — so the mechanism is present in this contract and simply unused here. *The rate and the clock are equally free.* `rate_per_second` and `time_elapsed` are witnesses; `:51-63` verifies `interest_amount = floor(old_total_debt * rate_second * time_elapsed / 315360000000)` by quotient-remainder, which is satisfiable for any `interest_amount` by choosing the rate to suit it. The contract stores both of the inputs the accrual should be derived from — `base_rate` (the constant `CDP_BASE_RATE = 500`, written at `:128`) and `CDP_LAST_INTEREST_UPDATE_KEY` — and the second is **written once at init to `0` (`:203`) and never read or updated anywhere in the tree**, so the elapsed time the accrual needs has no recorded source at all. *What is actually enforced* is two comparisons: `params.old_total_debt == stored` (`:1553`) and `params.new_total_debt - params.old_total_debt == params.interest_amount` (`:1566`). Both are the prover's to satisfy for any chosen `new_total_debt >= stored`. *The demonstrated consequence.* Any account can therefore submit `AccrueInterestV1` with a proof it can make itself and set `CDP_TOTAL_DEBT_KEY` to any value at or above its current one. That key is ordinary reading away from value, and both consumers were read in the same pass: `total_debt` is the denominator of the redemption exchange rate (`collateral_return = redeem_amount * total_collateral / total_debt`, `:1701`) and the input to the liquidation gate (`collateral_ratio = total_collateral * 10000 / total_debt`, compared at `:1279`). So the write controls both how much collateral a stablecoin redeems for and whether the pool is liquidatable at all. `CDP_ACCUMULATED_FEES_KEY` is written by the same path (`:1581`, `:1603`) and read by nothing today. *Why H and not C.* The two consumers above are read and named; the **payout** side of `LiquidateV1` — who funds the child transfer that pays the liquidator — is one file away and unread, and this register does not rank a value path it has not walked. If that read shows the pool funds it, this is C and belongs in Band 1. *The repair needs both halves, in this order, and a host-side comparison alone would be a no-op*: compare `params.accumulator_pub` against the point stored at init, **after** the circuit instances `accumulator_public_x/y`, because until the pair is exposed the value being compared is prover-supplied and a caller would simply copy the stored authority out of chain state — which is exactly the two halves `OBL-Z14`'s fix is made of. The rate and elapsed time need their own half on the host side (`base_rate` against the config, `time_elapsed` against a maintained `CDP_LAST_INTEREST_UPDATE_KEY`, written in `apply_accrue_interest_update`), because authenticating the caller does not make a free rate safe: the authority itself would still be able to write arbitrary debt. Stablecoin is not genesis, so all of it is pin-neutral **The payout read this row held its severity open for is done, and the answer holds it at H rather than C. *The child arms.* `MintStableV1` (`:1033-1108`) and `LiquidateV1` (`:1229-1337`) each require **exactly one** child call (`children_indexes.len() != 1` → reject) and it must be `promissory_note::transfer_v1`, opcode `0x04`. *The funding side of that child, which is the half this row named as unread.* Every input of a `transfer_v1` carries its own `Revoke_V2` proof: `promissory_note/proof/revoke.zk:47-72` derives `nullifier = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_NULLIFIER, spend_secret, coin)` with `coin = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_COMMITMENT, poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_SIGNATURE_SECRET, spend_secret), value, asset_id, coin_spend_hook, user_data, commitment_blind)` and constrains both the nullifier and `merkle_root(leaf_pos, path, coin_incl)` — so an input can only be supplied by a party that knows that note's `spend_secret`, and there is no delegation path in the circuit. The entries are then summed by `verify_value_conservation` (`promissory_note/src/entrypoint/mod.rs:516-557`, called at `:822`), which requires **every `token_commit` present in the outputs to appear in the inputs with an equal Pedersen sum and forbids any token type that appears only in the outputs** — so a transfer cannot exchange one token type for another and cannot create value. *So the pool does not fund it.* The stablecoin parent contributes nothing a child can draw on: all three arms' `process_*` return an update that writes ledger totals and nothing else, and **no stablecoin client builds the child transfer at all** — `grep -rln TransferParamsV1 src/contract/stablecoin/src/` returns `entrypoint.rs` alone, which merely decodes the child it is given. The payout is therefore bounded by notes the transaction already owns, and an unauthorized write to `CDP_TOTAL_DEBT_KEY` cannot convert into created or stolen value through this arm. **H, not C, and the reason is now a read rather than a deferral.** The consequence remains exactly as filed: `total_debt` is the denominator of the redemption exchange rate (`:1701`) and the input to the liquidation gate (`:1279`). What the same read did find is a separate row — the redeem arm, where the ledger is decremented against a payout that is zero-value by circuit: `OBL-C92` **The authorization half landed 2026-09-23, and the clause it does not reach is blocked on a design decision rather than on work.** *Two clauses, and the proposition has to be read as both.* The authorization clause — the accrual is authorized by the authority registered at init — is **now enforced**.
