The determination clause — the interest is determined by the contract's configured rate and the elapsed time since the last accrual — is **not**, and cannot be made so by the repair this cell named, for a reason the same cell's prescription does not contain. *What landed, three sides, in the order this row gave.* (i) `accrue_interest.zk`: the circuit already derived the accumulator point from the secret and compared it with `accumulator_pub_x/y`, but **neither operand was exposed**, so the equalities were between two prover-chosen values. It now instances the params pair (`constrain_instance(accumulator_pub_x); constrain_instance(accumulator_pub_y);`) — the same shape and the same reason as `governance_report.zk`'s `reporter_pub_x/y` one circuit over, which is the `OBL-Z14` precedent ported rather than reinvented. The checker classifies both as **bound** by the equality pair above them (`check-circuit-instance-derivation.sh`: `accrue_interest.zk 5 bound:2, declared-free:1, derived:1, redundant:1`; `unclassified` stays 11). (ii) The metadata arm publishes the pair first, in the circuit's instance order, refusing the identity by name — gate 2 reads the strongest form, **5 constrain_instance, 5 metadata pushes, 5 client public inputs**, which checks the client's `to_vec` against the circuit as well. (iii) `process_accrue_interest_instruction` reads the point stored at init (`STABLECOIN_CONTRACT_GOVERNANCE_PUBKEY_KEY`, written only at `init_contract`) and refuses any other, in the exact shape of the governance check this cell pointed at. The circuit and artifact were rebuilt (`make -C src/contract/stablecoin clean all`); stablecoin is fresh in `check-artifact-freshness.sh` and is not genesis, so nothing moved the pin. *The control, and it is two-sided by construction.* The spec gained `AccrueInterestV1ByStranger` immediately after its positive endpoint, with **every field identical except the accumulator secret** — including `old_total_debt`, which is why a rejection cannot be a statement about state. Run: `DWOW_TEST_LOGS=1 cargo test --release -p dwowd --all-features --lib test_heavyweight_stablecoin -- --test-threads 4` → **ok, 564.44s**, and the log carries the pair this needs: `AccrueInterest: old_debt=500, new_debt=500, interest=0` accepted for the registered key, followed for the stranger by *"AccrueInterest: accumulator is not the registered authority"*. The refusal is that check and not a proof-verification failure — the accrual's own log line precedes it — and the positive endpoint succeeding in the same run is what rules out a blanket refusal. Before the fix that endpoint had nothing to compare and accepted. *What the residual is, and why the cell's own prescription does not close it.* This cell says the rate and the clock "need their own half on the host side (`base_rate` against the config, `time_elapsed` against a maintained `CDP_LAST_INTEREST_UPDATE_KEY`)". Read against the code, that prescription has two prerequisites it does not name and one hole it does not close. The prerequisites: **the configured rate is stored nowhere** — `init_contract` writes five config keys (`total_debt`, `total_collateral`, `accumulated_fees`, `last_interest_update`, `total_redeemed`) and no rate; `params.base_rate` is consumed into `InitializeParams` and never persisted; and **there is no clock to maintain** — `CDP_LAST_INTEREST_UPDATE_KEY` is written once to `0` (`:203`) and read nowhere. The hole: `interest = debt × rate × elapsed / DENOM`, so **a checked rate with a caller-chosen period is cosmetic** — the caller sets `time_elapsed` to whatever makes the interest it wants, which is the same freedom the row is about. And the period cannot simply be required to equal the host's own computation, because **the client cannot know the block's timestamp**: a transaction is built before the block it lands in, so `params.time_elapsed == now − last` is unsatisfiable by any honest client. The coherent alternative is therefore not a check but a change of who computes: the host derives the period from the chain clock (`wasm::util::get_blockchain_time`) and the maintained stamp, computes the interest itself with checked arithmetic, and carries both in the update — which makes the params' `rate_per_second`, `time_elapsed` and `interest_amount` inert or removable, i.e. **an interface change to `AccrueInterestParams`** and to its client, not a two-line hardening. That is a design decision this row records rather than takes, and it is the reason the clause stays unmet. *Severity `H` → `M` on the register's own bar*, which asks whether a path from an untrusted input to the consequence can be stated: after this change the accrual requires the secret behind the point registered at init, so the demonstrated harm path — *"any account can submit `AccrueInterestV1` with a proof it can make itself"* — is gone, and what remains is the authority writing debt the way its own `UpdateConfig` already lets it set the rate. *One observation in the same arm, recorded and not filed as a defect*: the arm still publishes a **constant** tx pair (`poseidon_hash([3, 0, 0])` and zero), which matches the circuit's derivation only because the client's `AccrueInterestCallData::new` leaves `tx_commitment` and `tx_nonce` at zero — the reviewed "left zero by convention" shape this register already names for `tx_nonce`, and consistent on both sides rather than a `OBL-C78` literal. *Residue this fix did not touch, in the same contract and named here so it is not read as closed*: `OBL-C83` (the mint/liquidate arms record commitments that bind nothing) and `OBL-C92` (the redeem arm writes the ledger down against a zero-value payout) are unchanged |
| OBL-C83 | **OPEN** | A stablecoin position operation is bound to the position it acts on — the commitment the host records is the one the proof established, and an operation that consumes a position consumes it once | `mint_stable.zk:48,51,57,63`; `liquidate.zk:50,60` and `:42-44`; `stablecoin/src/entrypoint.rs:1116-1117`, `:1345`; `stablecoin/src/client/mint_stable.rs:47-53`; `stablecoin/src/model/mod.rs:498-505` | M | **Found 2026-09-23 in the same `OBL-Z18` slice, from the six non-resolving candidates that remain after `OBL-C82`, plus the two consumers their consequence runs into.** *The vacuity, measured on both sides.* In `mint_stable.zk` the exposed set is `nullifier_check` (`:43`), `tx_binding` (`:71`) and `tx_nonce` (`:72`); the four flagged equalities — `mint_check == new_debt` (`:48`), `old_collateral == new_collateral` (`:51`), `old_position == old_commitment` (`:57`), `new_position == new_commitment` (`:63`) — are each between two values that are neither exposed nor used anywhere else in the circuit, so each is satisfied by any witness. `liquidate.zk` is the same shape at `:50` and `:60`. The client states the consequence itself: the fields of `MintStablePublicInputs` are documented *"not a constrain_instance output"* (`client/mint_stable.rs:47-53`). *The one exposure that does reach the host does not repair it.* `nullifier_check = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_NULLIFIER, owner_secret, old_commitment)` (`mint_stable.zk:42-44`) binds the prover to `old_commitment` — but `old_commitment` is itself a free witness, and nothing verifies it is a position in any tree: there is no merkle path in the circuit (the witness list carries `position_root` and never uses it) and no tree read in the host. *The host records without comparing.* `apply_mint_stable_update` writes `params.mint_commitment` into the positions and stablecoin trees (`:1116-1117`) and compares it to nothing. *The stated property is unenforced on both sides.* `MintStableParams.proof` carries the doc comment *"ZK proof: mint doesn't violate pool collateralization"* and the struct carries `total_debt` / `total_collateral` *"for ratio check"* (`model/mod.rs:498-505`); `process_mint_stable_instruction` (`:1033-1108`) reads **neither field**, and `mint_stable.zk` contains no `less_than_or_equal` at all — unlike its four siblings, which each carry one (`open_position.zk:74`, `add_collateral.zk:80`, `remove_collateral.zk:80`, `repay_stable.zk:80`). *The same half-built model shows in liquidate's bookkeeping:* the nullifier that `liquidate.zk:42-44` exposes is consumed by nobody — `process_liquidate_instruction` never reads it and `apply_liquidate_update` (`:1345`) keys the liquidation record on `debt_covered.to_le_bytes()`, so two liquidations of the same size collide on one key and neither is marked spent. *Why M and not higher, stated as the read that is missing rather than as an assurance.* The unbound commitment and the absent ratio check are read on both sides, but what settles their consequence is the child call: both arms act through `promissory_note::transfer_v1`, and whether that transfer's **inputs** fund the mint or the liquidation is the half this slice did not read — so the value path is unranked and the severity is held at M. The next read is named: the PN transfer's input side for `MintStableV1` and `LiquidateV1`. These sites also sit inside the known V1-host/V2-circuit divergence (`v1-vs-v2-client-hazard`): the mint client documents a `BurnV1`/`MintV1` flow (`client/mint_stable.rs:26-33`) while the host implements `transfer_v1`, so the pair is mid-migration and part of this row's repair belongs to that work rather than beside it **The named read is done, and it holds this row at M.** Both arms act through `promissory_note::transfer_v1`, and that child cannot fund either operation with anything but notes the transaction already holds: every input carries a `Revoke_V2` proof (`promissory_note/proof/revoke.zk:47-72`, nullifier derived from the input's own `spend_secret`, merkle inclusion constrained) and `verify_value_conservation` (`promissory_note/src/entrypoint/mod.rs:516-557`) forces the output `token_commit` set to equal the input set with equal Pedersen sums. So an unbound `mint_commitment` or an absent ratio check cannot be turned into created value *through this contract's own arms* — the value path is bounded one layer down, which is why the severity does not move up. What remains true is everything the row already states and does not depend on that read: the host records a commitment it compares to nothing (`:1116-1117`), the four `mint_stable.zk` equalities are vacuous, `mint_stable.zk` carries no `less_than_or_equal` while its four siblings each do, and the liquidation record keys on `debt_covered.to_le_bytes()` so two liquidations of one size collide and neither marks the exposed nullifier spent (`:1345`). The read did produce one thing this row did not have: the same pass over the redeem arm found the ledger decremented against a payout that is zero-value by circuit, filed separately as `OBL-C92`, because that proposition — a redemption releases collateral — is not this one |
| OBL-C85 | **ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON** | The per-uncle Pedersen audit commitment `C_uncle_i = u_i·G_v + r_i·G_r` exists where the spec says it does — as EC points in the **stored cumulative supply chain** — so the subtractive mass balance `C_effective + Σ C_uncle_i = C_base` is auditable from durable state | `uncle_merkle.md` §Coinbase Split ("Pedersen audit commitments"); found by `OBL-C26`'s deletion | M | **Found 2026-09-23 while deciding what to do with `OBL-C26`'s dead cache: the spec's storage location for these points is not implemented anywhere. The disposition — accepted with reason, no `CumulativeSupplyEntry` change and no write path — is at the end of this cell.** Measured, both sides: `uncle_merkle.md:437-442` says the audit commitments are *"EC points in the stored cumulative supply chain (`blockchain.get_cumulative_supply`)"* and that they are **not spendable** — "no nullifier, no note, no merkle path attaches to them" — while `CumulativeSupplyEntry` (`src/linear/src/supply_chain.rs`) carries exactly one **aggregate** `value_commit` per height plus a plaintext `total_supply`, and `verify_uncle_split` checks the split as **integer arithmetic** (`canonical + Σ pin == base`), not as a commitment equation. So the *mass balance* is enforced on the plaintext amounts while the per-uncle points that the spec calls the audit trail are materialised nowhere durable: the only code that ever computed them was the in-memory `uncle_commitment_set` deleted under `OBL-C26`, and that was never read and never persisted. Severity M and not C: nothing here mints or destroys value — the split itself is enforced — the gap is that a property the spec states is auditable from stored state is not. **Not fixed here and not deferred silently**: fixing it means adding per-uncle points to the stored entry, i.e. a `CumulativeSupplyEntry` format change and the write path that fills it, which is a design change to stored state rather than a repair, and it belongs with whoever decides whether the audit trail is per-uncle or aggregate. Until then the honest reading of `get_cumulative_supply` is "the aggregate chain, reconciled against the schedule by `OBL-C45`" — not "the per-uncle audit commitments the spec describes" **RESTATED 2026-09-23: the first clause is false, the second is true by a mechanism the spec names two sections later, and the repair is a specification correction rather than a stored-format change — which is a smaller outcome than this row assumed and is reached by reading the spec's other half rather than by deciding the property is unachievable.** *What is false:* `uncle_merkle.md` §Two-commitment distinction said the points "are EC points in the stored cumulative supply chain (`blockchain.get_cumulative_supply`)", and the file's own status table claimed "Pedersen uncle commitment precompute — Implemented — `chain_state.rs::connect_block()` pre-computes `C_uncle_i = pedersen_commitment_u64(u_i, Blind(r_i))`" and "Deterministic uncle blind `r_i` — Implemented". Both are wrong: `CumulativeSupplyEntry` carries one aggregate `value_commit` and a plaintext `total_supply`, `verify_uncle_split` checks `canonical + Σ pin == base` as integers, and `from_uniform_bytes` has **no occurrence in `src/linear/src/`** — the formula has no implementation at all, not merely no storage. Three claims corrected in that file. *What is true, and this is the restatement:* §Audit Compatibility says the split "is auditable because `r_i` is deterministic — an auditor can recompute every `C_uncle_i` and verify `C_effective + sum(C_uncle_i) == C_base` for every block independently." Every input to that recomputation is durable: `uncle_hash = blake3(to_mining_blob())` from the uncle's stored header, `u_i = pin_confirmed_i` persisted in `UncleBlock` (`block.rs:184-200`), `H` the canonical height, and `C_base` the cumulative supply chain's own `value_commit`. So the property the row asks for — *auditable from durable state* — **holds**, by recomputation rather than by stored points, and the equation's own direction makes storage redundant: `C_effective := C_base − Σ C_uncle_i` is satisfied by construction, so a stored copy could only be tamper-evidence for a tautology, while a *recomputed* copy binds `u_i` to the uncle identity and height that produced it. *The decision, made rather than left open:* no `CumulativeSupplyEntry` format change and no write path. That would have been stored state added to carry a value the chain does not consult, in service of an audit that already works from what the chain does store. The three false spec claims were corrected instead, and `uncle_merkle.md` now says plainly that nothing computes `r_i` today. Severity stays M: the gap was never that value could be minted or destroyed — the split is enforced — it was that a stated audit path described storage that did not exist, and a specification that overstates its own implementation is exactly what this register exists to find |
| OBL-C86 | **CLOSED** | A record field named for the holder carries the holder | `identity/src/entrypoint.rs:681`; found while re-reading `OBL-Z17` | M | **NEW 2026-09-23, split out of `OBL-Z17`'s re-read rather than left inside it, because it is a different proposition.** The verify path builds `VerifyCapabilityUpdateV1 { capability_id, holder_pub: params.capability_proof.issuer_pub, verified: true }` — the **issuer's** key under a field named `holder_pub`. Both sides read: the circuit carries `holder_pub_x/y` as witnesses that enter `credential_data` (`verify_capability.zk:80-90`) and attaches them to the credential, but **does not expose them** — correctly, since disclosing the holder to a verifier is the privacy break the whole check is built to avoid — while the stored `Credential` also carries a `holder_pub` (`model/mod.rs:245-254`) that the verify path never compares against anything. So the update states one principal and names another. **Why M and not L**: nothing reads it *today* — `apply_verify_capability_update` writes no state (`entrypoint.rs:689-692`) and no consumer contract reads a child call's return data (they check the call's byte, its target contract id, and their own proof's emptiness) — so there is no demonstrated harm, and this register does not rank an undemonstrated one. What it is, is an authorization datum in the primitive that gates every genesis contract's auth, labelled as its own opposite: the first consumer to trust `update.holder_pub` would learn the issuer. **The repair is one line and it is decided rather than offered**: `holder_pub: credential.holder_pub` — the value the name declares, read from the stored record the function has already loaded and whose commitment the proof was just required to match (`:635-638`). Disclosure to the caller is not the privacy break the circuit avoids: the update is what `process_update` returns to the caller that produced the proof, `apply` persists nothing (`:689-692`), and the circuit's own choice not to expose the holder is what keeps the *verifier* from learning it. Renaming the field instead was rejected because the holder is the datum the record exists to report. It is in a genesis contract, so it lands with the batched re-roll rather than as a drive-by edit **CLOSED 2026-09-23 — one line, `holder_pub: credential.holder_pub`, landed in the batch with `OBL-C62` and `OBL-Z19`.** The record it reads from is the one the function had already loaded and whose commitment the proof was just required to match, so no new lookup and no new failure mode. Verified by the contract rebuilding clean (`make -C src/contract/identity clean && make all`) and by `scripts/check-artifact-freshness.sh` → 32 artefacts fresh. What is **not** claimed: that the field is now read by anyone — it still is not, which is why this closed as M and not as a behaviour change. `identity` is genesis contract 4, so the fix moved the pin in the same re-roll. **And the contract was re-run end to end rather than only rebuilt**: `DWOW_TEST_LOGS=1 RUST_MIN_STACK=67108864 cargo test --release -p dwowd --all-features test_heavyweight_identity -- --test-threads 4` → **1 passed, 215.72s**, which drives issue-credential, register-capability, issue-capability and verify-capability through the real client against the real circuits. The field this row is about is not asserted by that test — nothing reads it, which is the row's own point — so what the run establishes is that the change is not a regression, not that the value is observed. **Re-measured on a later tree: 1 passed, 225.16s**, after a concurrent session's `src/sdk` edit — see `OBL-Z19`'s note on why a green figure carries the tree it was taken on |
| OBL-C87 | **CLOSED** | A test's post-condition and the code it checks agree on the value, and a red heavyweight test is a finding rather than noise | `bin/dwowd/src/tests/specs/purse_spec.rs` (WithdrawV1 `verify_state`); found by running the test, not by reading it | L | **NEW AND CLOSED 2026-09-23 — a red heavyweight test that the register had never mentioned, and whose cause turned out to be the test.** `test_heavyweight_purse` fails with, verbatim, `Error: Custom("TEST-FAIL [purse::WithdrawV1]: verify_state failed — WARN [purse::WithdrawV1]: nullifier must exist after withdrawal")`, measured with `DWOW_TEST_LOGS=1` (91.34s) — which is why the tag was worth re-measuring rather than trusting: the `WARN [purse::WithdrawV1]` prefix is not the contract's output. It is the *test's own* error string (`purse_spec.rs:77`), so the recorded "cause" was a post-condition failing with no stated cause anywhere, exactly the shape `OBL-C76`'s note describes. **Both sides printed, and the spec was the wrong one.** The producer is `harness/purse.rs:89-91`: the withdraw proof is built with `os = 42` and `sn = 1`, so `nf = poseidon_hash([dnl=1, os=42, pid=1, sn=1])`, and `apply` writes exactly that — `entrypoint/mod.rs:215-216`, `db_mark_spent(ndb, &u.nullifier.to_bytes())`, where `Nullifier::to_bytes()` is `to_repr()` and `from_bytes` applies no domain hash, so the key is the value. So the contract was right and its expectation was stale, which is the outcome that makes this severity L rather than a production finding: nothing mints, nothing is unverified, and the test was reporting a defect in itself. **Fixed by taking the value from the producer rather than recalling it** — the spec now computes `poseidon_hash([1, 42, 1, 1])` and says where each operand came from, so the next reader does not have to re-derive the pair. *Negative control, measured on both sides of the same test*: with the stale constants the run fails at this assertion after 91.34s; with the producer's constants it is **1 passed, 190.81s** (`DWOW_TEST_LOGS=1 RUST_MIN_STACK=67108864 cargo test --release --all-features test_heavyweight_purse -- --test-threads 4`). **Re-measured on a later tree: 1 passed, 200.51s** — after a concurrent session's `src/sdk` edit, which links into every contract. Both figures are kept so the green one carries the tree it was taken on | L |

Severity L: nothing is unverified and nothing is forgeable; what is lost is a reader's ability to tell which fields matter | L |
